Um… I guess I came in because I just don’t really care about things anymore. Not like I used to. And game night too, I keep meaning to go, but it all just sits there in my head like chores I’m avoiding.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not sad exactly all the time, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling under everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll be doing something normal, like folding laundry or making coffee, and it’s just there. And then I start thinking I should clean the fridge, because I bought vegetables and forgot about them again, and then I feel bad about that too… but, um, yeah. Mostly it’s that feeling underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I haven’t been checking, honestly. My scale is under the sink somewhere, I think, behind all the cleaning stuff I bought and then didn’t really use.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… about the same? I mean, jeans are always weird depending on whether they just came out of the dryer. So I haven’t noticed anything big, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than you used to or feeling restless and fidgety?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I don’t think I’m walking around super slow or anything… but I’m also not exactly paying attention to that.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit and pick at my nails or move stuff around on the coffee table, but then I do that when I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill too, so I don’t know if that counts.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. Not always about one specific thing, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling sitting underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even if nothing is actually happening.

It’s stupid little stuff too, like if I don’t answer a text right away, or if I leave dishes in the sink, I’ll feel like I did something wrong. Which—before, I mean, I wasn’t perfect, nobody is. My aunt used to leave coffee mugs everywhere and called it “decorating,” so I guess dishes shouldn’t feel like a moral failure. But lately it just kind of does.

So… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.

I used to go to dance class, and I liked hiking, and I’d try new recipes—my kitchen used to be a mess after, like, in a good way. And game night too, with friends… though honestly they get too competitive sometimes.\n\nBut now all of it just feels like chores I keep putting off. Like things that used to make me feel like me are just sitting there, and I can’t make myself want them.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things, but just can't seem to motivate yourself to do them, or have you actually lost interest in the things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: It’s more like I’ve actually lost interest, I think. Like, I’ll look at my dance shoes or think about hiking and it’s not that I’m excited but can’t get up—it’s just… there’s no pull there anymore.\n\nEven cooking, which I used to make a whole thing out of—music on, trying some recipe I found online, making way too many dishes—now it just feels like another task. And game night too, I keep meaning to go, but it all just sits there in my head like chores I’m avoiding.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not sad exactly all the time, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling under everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll be doing something normal, like folding laundry or making coffee, and it’s just there. And then I start thinking I should clean the fridge, because I bought vegetables and forgot about them again, and then I feel bad about that too… but, um, yeah. Mostly it’s that feeling underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I haven’t been checking, honestly. My scale is under the sink somewhere, I think, behind all the cleaning stuff I bought and then didn’t really use.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… about the same? I mean, jeans are always weird depending on whether they just came out of the dryer. So I haven’t noticed anything big, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than you used to or feeling restless and fidgety?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I don’t think I’m walking around super slow or anything… but I’m also not exactly paying attention to that.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit and pick at my nails or move stuff around on the coffee table, but then I do that when I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill too, so I don’t know if that counts. My neighbor’s dog was barking for like an hour yesterday and I kept getting up to look out the window, but that might just be because it was annoying.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change either way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. Like, hurting myself sounds so specific, and I don’t think I’ve been planning anything or—sorry, I’m trying to think.\n\nSometimes when people ask that, my mind kind of blanks out, like when you’re at the grocery store and someone asks what you came in for and suddenly you can’t remember, even though you had a whole list at home. I did that last week and came back with paper towels but no milk.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I have a clear answer beyond that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since that robbery at the festival job. That was, what, about two years and three months ago now.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember little stuff from that day, like the folding table we had and how sticky the ground was from spilled lemonade, but then the time since then kind of blurs together. But yeah… that’s when it started, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not always about one specific thing, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling sitting underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even if nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s stupid little stuff too, like if I don’t answer a text right away, or if I leave dishes in the sink, I’ll feel like I did something wrong. Which—before, I mean, I wasn’t perfect, nobody is. My aunt used to leave coffee mugs everywhere and called it “decorating,” so I guess dishes shouldn’t feel like a moral failure. But lately it just kind of does.\n\nSo… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy spending time with friends, or has that also lost its appeal for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… friends too, I guess. I mean, I still like them, it’s not that they did anything. But game night, which used to be this thing I looked forward to—like we had this whole argument once about whether house rules in Uno are basically illegal, and it was funny for weeks—now it just feels like another thing I have to make myself go to.\n\nIt’s kind of the same with dance class and hiking and cooking. I used to try new recipes just because, you know, chopping vegetables felt sort of calming, and now even that feels like a chore sitting there waiting for me. So… yeah, the stuff that used to make me feel like me doesn’t really pull me in anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like hearing voices, no. Sometimes I get kind of jumpy and my mind fills in things, like if the house makes a noise I’ll be sure someone’s there for a second, but then it’s just the fridge or the pipes or whatever.\n\nMy old apartment had this heater that clicked like someone tapping a spoon on a glass, and I used to hate that sound—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I see things other people don’t see or hear voices talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble sitting still or that you're fidgeting a lot when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly. I mean, I’ll move around sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big thing when I’m trying to relax.\n\nActually, I say “relax” and I’m thinking about how my couch has this one spring that kind of pokes up if you sit in the wrong spot, so maybe nobody could sit still on that thing. I keep meaning to replace it, but then I start looking at furniture online and it turns into this whole weird maze of beige sofas.\n\nBut, sorry—your question. I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m restless like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I did that last week and came back with paper towels but no milk.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I have a clear answer beyond that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since that robbery at the festival job. That was, what, about two years and three months ago now.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember little stuff from that day, like the folding table we had and how sticky the ground was from spilled lemonade, but then the time since then kind of blurs together. But yeah… that’s when it started, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not always about one specific thing, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling sitting underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even if nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s stupid little stuff too, like if I don’t answer a text right away, or if I leave dishes in the sink, I’ll feel like I did something wrong. Which—before, I mean, I wasn’t perfect, nobody is. My aunt used to leave coffee mugs everywhere and called it “decorating,” so I guess dishes shouldn’t feel like a moral failure. But lately it just kind of does.\n\nSo… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy spending time with friends, or has that also lost its appeal for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… friends too, I guess. But no, I don’t think I see things other people don’t see or hear voices talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble sitting still or that you're fidgeting a lot when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly. I mean, I’ll move around sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big thing when I’m trying to relax.\n\nActually, I say “relax” and I’m thinking about how my couch has this one spring that kind of pokes up if you sit in the wrong spot, so maybe nobody could sit still on that thing. I keep meaning to replace it, but then I start looking at furniture online and it turns into this whole weird maze of beige sofas.\n\nBut, sorry—your question. I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself much… I used to have a scale in the bathroom but the battery died, and then I kept forgetting to replace it. I don’t sit and think, “Oh, I’m fidgeting,” you know? Sometimes I’ll bounce my foot or pick at a thread on my sleeve, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal.\n\nIt’s funny, my mom used to tell me not to jiggle my leg at the dinner table because it shook the whole table, especially if there was a glass of water—sorry, that’s not really helpful. I mean, I don’t think I’m walking around super slow or anything… but I’m also not exactly paying attention to that.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit and pick at my nails or move stuff around on the coffee table, but then I do that when I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill too, so I don’t know if that counts. My neighbor’s dog was barking for like an hour yesterday and I kept getting up to look out the window, but that might just be because it was annoying.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change either way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. Like, hurting myself sounds so specific, and I don’t think I’ve been planning anything or—sorry, I’m trying to think.\n\nSometimes when people ask that, my mind kind of blanks out, like when you’re at the grocery store and someone asks what you came in for and suddenly you can’t remember, even though you had a whole list at home. I did that last week and came back with paper towels but no milk.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I have a clear answer beyond that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since that robbery at the festival job. That was, what, about two years and three months ago now.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember little stuff from that day, like the folding table we had and how sticky the ground was from spilled lemonade, but then the time since then kind of blurs together. But yeah… that’s when it started, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not always about one specific thing, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling sitting underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even if nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s stupid little stuff too, like if I don’t answer a text right away, or if I leave dishes in the sink, I’ll feel like I did something wrong. Which—before, I mean, I wasn’t perfect, nobody is. My aunt used to leave coffee mugs everywhere and called it “decorating,” so I guess dishes shouldn’t feel like a moral failure. But lately it just kind of does.\n\nSo… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy spending time with friends, or has that also lost its appeal for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… friends too, I guess. I mean, I still like them, it’s not that they did anything. But game night, which used to be this thing I looked forward to—like we had this whole argument once about whether house rules in Uno are basically illegal, and it was funny for weeks—now it just feels like another thing I have to make myself go to.\n\nIt’s kind of the same with dance class and hiking and cooking. I used to try new recipes just because, you know, chopping vegetables felt sort of calming, and now even that feels like a chore sitting there waiting for me. So… yeah, the stuff that used to make me feel like me doesn’t really pull me in anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like hearing voices, no. Sometimes I get kind of jumpy and my mind fills in things, like if the house makes a noise I’ll be sure someone’s there for a second, but then it’s just the fridge or the pipes or whatever.\n\nMy old apartment had this heater that clicked like someone tapping a spoon on a glass, and I used to hate that sound—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I see things other people don’t see or hear voices talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble sitting still or that you're fidgeting a lot when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly. I mean, I’ll move around sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big thing when I’m trying to relax.\n\nActually, I say “relax” and I’m thinking about how my couch has this one spring that kind of pokes up if you sit in the wrong spot, so maybe nobody could sit still on that thing. I keep meaning to replace it, but then I start looking at furniture online and it turns into this whole weird maze of beige sofas.\n\nBut, sorry—your question. I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself much… I used to have a scale in the bathroom but the battery died, and then I kept forgetting to replace it. It’s one of those little round batteries, which somehow are never in the drawer when you need them.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I’ve lost a significant amount. My clothes seem mostly the same, I guess. I haven’t really been tracking it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe a little? I’m not really sure. I don’t sit and think, “Oh, I’m fidgeting,” you know? Sometimes I’ll bounce my foot or pick at a thread on my sleeve, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal.\n\nIt’s funny, my mom used to tell me not to jiggle my leg at the dinner table because it shook the whole table, especially if there was a glass of water—sorry, that’s not really helpful. I haven’t really been paying attention to it lately, so I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, sometimes when people ask it so directly I get kind of stuck, like I’m trying to figure out what counts as a “thought” versus just… having a bad moment, you know?\n\nSorry, that probably doesn’t answer it. I haven’t really… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve sat down and thought about it in that exact way. It’s just hard to sort through sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I’m still not sure how to say it. I don’t think I’ve had, like, a clear thought of “I want to end my life.” It’s more that when you ask it, my brain kind of freezes and starts sorting through every bad feeling I’ve had, like I’m looking for the right file in a drawer.\n\nSorry, that’s a weird way to put it. I guess I don’t have a definite answer beyond… I don’t think so, not in that direct way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, sleep has always been kind of… I don’t know, it depends. Some nights I stay up too late, but sometimes that’s because I get caught watching stupid videos or I start folding laundry at midnight for no reason.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it a significant change. I haven’t really been tracking it or anything. Like, my friend has one of those watches that tells you your sleep score, and she’s always talking about it, which honestly would probably make me more stressed. But yeah… I can’t say I’ve noticed a big clear change.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… maybe sometimes? Like, I worry I’m taking up too much space, or making things harder, but then I also don’t know if that’s the same as feeling like a burden.

It’s funny, my aunt used to say people should just “ask for help when they need it,” but she’d also complain for an hour if someone asked her to pick them up from the airport, so I think I learned to get weird about needing anything. Sorry, that’s kind of off topic.

I guess I’m not sure how much I feel like a burden versus just… not wanting to bother people.

My neighbor’s dog was barking for like an hour yesterday and I kept getting up to look out the window, but that might just be because it was annoying.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change either way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. Like, hurting myself sounds so specific, and I don’t think I’ve been planning anything or—sorry, I’m trying to think.\n\nSometimes when people ask that, my mind kind of blanks out, like when you’re at the grocery store and someone asks what you came in for and suddenly you can’t remember, even though you had a whole list at home. I did that last week and came back with paper towels but no milk.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I have a clear answer beyond that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since that robbery at the festival job. That was, what, about two years and three months ago now.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember little stuff from that day, like the folding table we had and how sticky the ground was from spilled lemonade, but then the time since then kind of blurs together. But yeah… that’s when it started, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not always about one specific thing, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling sitting underneath everything. But lately it just kind of does.\n\nSo… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy spending time with friends, or has that also lost its appeal for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… friends too, I guess. I mean, I still like them, it’s not that they did anything. But game night, which used to be this thing I looked forward to—like we had this whole argument once about whether house rules in Uno are basically illegal, and it was funny for weeks—now it just feels like another thing I have to make myself go to.\n\nIt’s kind of the same with dance class and hiking and cooking. I used to try new recipes just because, you know, chopping vegetables felt sort of calming, and now even that feels like a chore sitting there waiting for me. So… yeah, the stuff that used to make me feel like me doesn’t really pull me in anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like hearing voices, no. Sometimes I get kind of jumpy and my mind fills in things, like if the house makes a noise I’ll be sure someone’s there for a second, but then it’s just the fridge or the pipes or whatever.\n\nMy old apartment had this heater that clicked like someone tapping a spoon on a glass, and I used to hate that sound—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I see things other people don’t see or hear voices talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble sitting still or that you're fidgeting a lot when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly. I mean, I’ll move around sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big thing when I’m trying to relax.\n\nActually, I say “relax” and I’m thinking about how my couch has this one spring that kind of pokes up if you sit in the wrong spot, so maybe nobody could sit still on that thing. I keep meaning to replace it, but then I start looking at furniture online and it turns into this whole weird maze of beige sofas.\n\nBut, sorry—your question. I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself much… I used to have a scale in the bathroom but the battery died, and then I kept forgetting to replace it. It’s one of those little round batteries, which somehow are never in the drawer when you need them.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I’ve lost a significant amount. My clothes seem mostly the same, I guess. I haven’t really been tracking it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe a little? I’m not really sure. I don’t sit and think, “Oh, I’m fidgeting,” you know? What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I came in because I just don’t really care about things anymore. Not like I used to. I used to go to dance class, and I liked hiking, and I’d try new recipes—my kitchen used to be a mess after, like, in a good way. And game night too, with friends… though honestly they get too competitive sometimes.\n\nBut now all of it just feels like chores I keep putting off. Like things that used to make me feel like me are just sitting there, and I can’t make myself want them.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things, but just can't seem to motivate yourself to do them, or have you actually lost interest in the things that used to bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: It’s more like I’ve actually lost interest, I think. Like, I’ll look at my dance shoes or think about hiking and it’s not that I’m excited but can’t get up—it’s just… there’s no pull there anymore.\n\nEven cooking, which I used to make a whole thing out of—music on, trying some recipe I found online, making way too many dishes—now it just feels like another task. And game night too, I keep meaning to go, but it all just sits there in my head like chores I’m avoiding.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not sad exactly all the time, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling under everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even when nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s weird because I’ll be doing something normal, like folding laundry or making coffee, and it’s just there. And then I start thinking I should clean the fridge, because I bought vegetables and forgot about them again, and then I feel bad about that too… but, um, yeah. Mostly it’s that feeling underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I haven’t been checking, honestly. My scale is under the sink somewhere, I think, behind all the cleaning stuff I bought and then didn’t really use.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel… about the same? I mean, jeans are always weird depending on whether they just came out of the dryer. So I haven’t noticed anything big, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than you used to or feeling restless and fidgety?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I don’t think I’m walking around super slow or anything… but I’m also not exactly paying attention to that.\n\nSometimes I’ll sit and pick at my nails or move stuff around on the coffee table, but then I do that when I’m trying to remember if I paid the electric bill too, so I don’t know if that counts. My neighbor’s dog was barking for like an hour yesterday and I kept getting up to look out the window, but that might just be because it was annoying.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change either way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. Like, hurting myself sounds so specific, and I don’t think I’ve been planning anything or—sorry, I’m trying to think.\n\nSometimes when people ask that, my mind kind of blanks out, like when you’re at the grocery store and someone asks what you came in for and suddenly you can’t remember, even though you had a whole list at home. I did that last week and came back with paper towels but no milk.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I have a clear answer beyond that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… since that robbery at the festival job. That was, what, about two years and three months ago now.\n\nIt’s weird because I remember little stuff from that day, like the folding table we had and how sticky the ground was from spilled lemonade, but then the time since then kind of blurs together. But yeah… that’s when it started, around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that you wouldn't have felt guilty about before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not always about one specific thing, but most days there’s this kind of low, sour feeling sitting underneath everything. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even if nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s stupid little stuff too, like if I don’t answer a text right away, or if I leave dishes in the sink, I’ll feel like I did something wrong. Which—before, I mean, I wasn’t perfect, nobody is. My aunt used to leave coffee mugs everywhere and called it “decorating,” so I guess dishes shouldn’t feel like a moral failure. But lately it just kind of does.\n\nSo… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy spending time with friends, or has that also lost its appeal for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… friends too, I guess. I mean, I still like them, it’s not that they did anything. But game night, which used to be this thing I looked forward to—like we had this whole argument once about whether house rules in Uno are basically illegal, and it was funny for weeks—now it just feels like another thing I have to make myself go to.\n\nIt’s kind of the same with dance class and hiking and cooking. I used to try new recipes just because, you know, chopping vegetables felt sort of calming, and now even that feels like a chore sitting there waiting for me. So… yeah, the stuff that used to make me feel like me doesn’t really pull me in anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like hearing voices, no. Sometimes I get kind of jumpy and my mind fills in things, like if the house makes a noise I’ll be sure someone’s there for a second, but then it’s just the fridge or the pipes or whatever.\n\nMy old apartment had this heater that clicked like someone tapping a spoon on a glass, and I used to hate that sound—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I see things other people don’t see or hear voices talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble sitting still or that you're fidgeting a lot when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly. I mean, I’ll move around sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big thing when I’m trying to relax.\n\nActually, I say “relax” and I’m thinking about how my couch has this one spring that kind of pokes up if you sit in the wrong spot, so maybe nobody could sit still on that thing. I keep meaning to replace it, but then I start looking at furniture online and it turns into this whole weird maze of beige sofas.\n\nBut, sorry—your question. I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself much… I used to have a scale in the bathroom but the battery died, and then I kept forgetting to replace it. It’s one of those little round batteries, which somehow are never in the drawer when you need them.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I’ve lost a significant amount. My clothes seem mostly the same, I guess. I haven’t really been tracking it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe a little? I’m not really sure. I don’t sit and think, “Oh, I’m fidgeting,” you know? Sometimes I’ll bounce my foot or pick at a thread on my sleeve, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal.\n\nIt’s funny, my mom used to tell me not to jiggle my leg at the dinner table because it shook the whole table, especially if there was a glass of water—sorry, that’s not really helpful. I haven’t really been paying attention to it lately, so I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, sometimes when people ask it so directly I get kind of stuck, like I’m trying to figure out what counts as a “thought” versus just… having a bad moment, you know?\n\nSorry, that probably doesn’t answer it. I haven’t really… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve sat down and thought about it in that exact way. It’s just hard to sort through sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I’m still not sure how to say it. I mean, jeans are always weird depending on whether they just came out of the dryer. Like fear and guilt mixed together, even if nothing is actually happening.\n\nIt’s stupid little stuff too, like if I don’t answer a text right away, or if I leave dishes in the sink, I’ll feel like I did something wrong. But lately it just kind of does.\n\nSo… yeah, I feel guilty more than I used to, even when I can’t really point to a good reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy spending time with friends, or has that also lost its appeal for you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… friends too, I guess. I mean, I still like them, it’s not that they did anything. But game night, which used to be this thing I looked forward to—like we had this whole argument once about whether house rules in Uno are basically illegal, and it was funny for weeks—now it just feels like another thing I have to make myself go to.\n\nIt’s kind of the same with dance class and hiking and cooking. I used to try new recipes just because, you know, chopping vegetables felt sort of calming, and now even that feels like a chore sitting there waiting for me. So… yeah, the stuff that used to make me feel like me doesn’t really pull me in anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that others can't?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not like hearing voices, no. Sometimes I get kind of jumpy and my mind fills in things, like if the house makes a noise I’ll be sure someone’s there for a second, but then it’s just the fridge or the pipes or whatever.\n\nMy old apartment had this heater that clicked like someone tapping a spoon on a glass, and I used to hate that sound—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But no, I don’t think I see things other people don’t see or hear voices talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble sitting still or that you're fidgeting a lot when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it fidgeting exactly. I mean, I’ll move around sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as a big thing when I’m trying to relax.\n\nActually, I say “relax” and I’m thinking about how my couch has this one spring that kind of pokes up if you sit in the wrong spot, so maybe nobody could sit still on that thing. I keep meaning to replace it, but then I start looking at furniture online and it turns into this whole weird maze of beige sofas.\n\nBut, sorry—your question. I’m not sure. I haven’t really paid attention to whether I’m restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t weigh myself much… I used to have a scale in the bathroom but the battery died, and then I kept forgetting to replace it. It’s one of those little round batteries, which somehow are never in the drawer when you need them.\n\nBut, um… I don’t think I’ve lost a significant amount. My clothes seem mostly the same, I guess. I haven’t really been tracking it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe a little? I’m not really sure. I don’t sit and think, “Oh, I’m fidgeting,” you know? Sometimes I’ll bounce my foot or pick at a thread on my sleeve, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal.\n\nIt’s funny, my mom used to tell me not to jiggle my leg at the dinner table because it shook the whole table, especially if there was a glass of water—sorry, that’s not really helpful. I haven’t really been paying attention to it lately, so I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about ending your life?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, sometimes when people ask it so directly I get kind of stuck, like I’m trying to figure out what counts as a “thought” versus just… having a bad moment, you know?\n\nSorry, that probably doesn’t answer it. I haven’t really… I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve sat down and thought about it in that exact way. It’s just hard to sort through sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to end your life?\n\nPatient: I… I’m still not sure how to say it. I don’t think I’ve had, like, a clear thought of “I want to end my life.” It’s more that when you ask it, my brain kind of freezes and starts sorting through every bad feeling I’ve had, like I’m looking for the right file in a drawer.\n\nSorry, that’s a weird way to put it. I guess I don’t have a definite answer beyond… I don’t think so, not in that direct way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, sleep has always been kind of… I don’t know, it depends. Some nights I stay up too late, but sometimes that’s because I get caught watching stupid videos or I start folding laundry at midnight for no reason.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d call it a significant change. I haven’t really been tracking it or anything. Like, my friend has one of those watches that tells you your sleep score, and she’s always talking about it, which honestly would probably make me more stressed. But yeah… I can’t say I’ve noticed a big clear change.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a burden to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes? Like, I worry I’m taking up too much space, or making things harder, but then I also don’t know if that’s the same as feeling like a burden.\n\nIt’s funny, my aunt used to say people should just “ask for help when they need it,” but she’d also complain for an hour if someone asked her to pick them up from the airport, so I think I learned to get weird about needing anything. Sorry, that’s kind of off topic.\n\nI guess I’m not sure how much I feel like a burden versus just… not wanting to bother people.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past two years where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: Um… better for a couple months? I don’t think so, not really in a clear way. This has kind of been going on since the robbery at that festival job, which was… about two years and three months ago now, I think.\n\nThere might’ve been weeks where I was distracted or things felt more normal, like around holidays or when my cousin was visiting and everyone was busy cooking way too much food, but I wouldn’t say a couple of months where I felt actually better. It’s been sort of… there, since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past two years where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not completely. I mean, there are days where I can act more normal, if that makes sense, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt like my old self again.\n\nIt’s been going on since that robbery at the festival job… about two years and three months ago now. Funny, I still remember random stuff from that job, like how the food truck next to us always ran out of lemonade way too early—but, yeah, since then I don’t think there’s been a stretch where I was just fully back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself unintentionally moving your body, like tapping your feet or hands, when you're not trying to?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed that much. Maybe I’ll fidget a little if I’m waiting somewhere, but I don’t know if it’s more than anybody else.\n\nLike, my cousin does this thing where she bounces her knee so hard the whole table shakes, and everyone gets annoyed at Thanksgiving—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. For me, I don’t think it’s a big thing, or at least nobody’s pointed it out to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
